## Researcher–agent collaboration



Chronological shared conversation. The researcher sees these handoffs, not private tool output. Full tool and model records follow.

### 1. Researcher — initial_request

Please inspect the staged repository and available data files, especially capital.csv and any analysis scripts/notebooks relevant to PCA in 100-year bins. Report what files and columns are available, identify missingness or data-structure issues relevant to the requested question, and propose a concise analysis workflow for determining symbolic-capital loadings on PC1 and their distribution on PC2 across time slices. State consequential assumptions or uncertainties (including PCA input/standardization and interpretation of component signs). Do not yet execute the full analysis or provide the final finding.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** initial_inspection; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The brief identifies capital.csv variables for symbolic capital (hatchets/swords, daggers, house size) and fixes 100-year bins, but supplies no observed PCA results or details of the exact preprocessing. Initial inspection should establish data availability and recommend an approach before authorizing analysis.

**Open objectives:** Inspect repository and data availability/structure; Propose PCA workflow and identify consequential assumptions or uncertainties; Determine data-grounded characterization of symbolic-capital PC1 values and PC2 distribution across 100-year slices

**Agent reply (plan)**

Inspection finds three data files and no staged analysis scripts/notebooks. `capital.csv` has 39 rows, 15 columns: BCE, group, color, and 12 capital indicators (`ZAxtSchwert`, `ZDolch`, `Zhausgr`, `ZCU_AU`, `Zamber`, `ZMonument`, `ZHort`, `ZSichel`, `ZBeil`, `ZMW`, `ZKeform`, `Zkeverz`). BCE runs from 4300 to 500 in 100-year increments (no duplicated BCE values). Every cell is populated, but the indicator columns are currently parsed as strings because decimals use commas. The two metadata columns group/color are described as dropped after loading and are not quantitative PCA inputs.

`time_series_data.csv` (3,601 × 37) has annual CE/calBP records with raw and interpolated versions of the capital indicators, among other series. Raw capital columns have 3,564 missing values apiece, while their `_inter` counterparts are complete—consistent with sparse source measurements plus interpolation. `pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv` has 4,084 × 13 rows and substantially sparse raw pollen PC1 columns; interpolated/smoothed series are complete. No scripts or notebooks are present in the inspected repository. Thus `capital.csv` appears to be the direct time-slice table relevant to the question, while the other files can provide cross-check/context rather than substitute silently for it.

Proposed concise workflow after approval: parse decimal commas in the capital indicators as numeric; exclude BCE/group/color; retain the 12 indicators and process each row/time slice independently. For each 100-year row, run PCA across the indicators (the relevant interpretation is variable loadings, not observation scores), after choosing a transparent scaling method. Record the PC1 loading for house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords and the PC2 loadings for those variables, then summarize signs, relative magnitudes/rank, and whether PC2 loadings are positive/negative or near zero across slices. Verify numeric conversion, per-slice sample/feature structure, component variance and loading normalization; compare standardized (correlation PCA) with unstandardized PCA if preprocessing is undocumented.

Assumptions/uncertainties: the dataset indicates all values are Z values, but it does not specify whether the original PCA used these directly, standardized each slice again, or used interpolated annual series. Since capital.csv is complete and indexed at 100-year intervals, it is the likely intended input, but the 12 indicators may be pre-standardized across the full chronology rather than per slice. With just one row per slice, a conventional PCA treating rows as observations within each slice is impossible; the plausible alternative is PCA on the 12 capital-form values within a slice, which yields loadings for the variables but has only one observation and is not statistically identified as conventional sample PCA. The question wording and data layout therefore leave the PCA unit/organization materially uncertain; analysis should check for relevant code or context if available. Component signs are mathematically arbitrary and may flip independently across slices, so report relative sign patterns only after a consistent sign convention (e.g. orient PC1 to positive sum of loadings) and note this convention. In addition, PCA across indicators using a single row per time slice may simply be an intended descriptive decomposition of the vector, rather than empirically estimated covariance.

**Shared evidence:**

Shell file inventory: only task.json and three data CSVs; no notebooks/scripts. pandas inspection: capital.csv shape 39x15; zero reported NaNs, 12 indicator fields string-parsed; BCE integers 500–4300 with no duplicates. time_series_data.csv shape 3601x37, raw capital fields each missing 3564, interpolated fields complete. Pollen file 4084x13 with raw PC1 columns highly sparse and interpolated series complete.

**Assumptions:** capital.csv is the likely dataset intended by the phrase forms of capital in hundred-year slices; Column descriptions and dataset metadata are accurate

**Uncertainties:** PCA sampling unit and whether it is per-slice, pooled, or based on original source observations are not specified in the staged assets; PCA standardization method is undocumented despite variables being Z values; Component sign orientation is arbitrary

### 2. Researcher — refine_analysis

Thank you. The key design issue is real: the 39-row capital table is one observation per century, so a separate conventional PCA within each century cannot estimate covariance. Please clarify the intended PCA construction from the available study context or data provenance if possible; in particular, whether the PCA is pooled across the 39 time slices using the 12 indicators, with scores/loadings then examined across slices, or instead uses underlying observations not staged here. Do not treat a PCA of a single 12-value row as a conventional per-slice PCA. If no further provenance is available, propose a defensible analysis that answers the question while explicitly distinguishing variable loadings from century-level scores and flagging the resulting limitation. Also retain a transparent scaling choice and note PCA sign arbitrariness.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** approach_review; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The inspection reports a complete 39-by-12 capital-indicator matrix at 100-year intervals, but only one row per time slice; this rules out conventional independent per-slice PCA. The proposed workflow correctly identified this uncertainty, which must be resolved or explicitly accommodated before main analysis.

**Open objectives:** Resolve PCA sampling unit and identify an analytically valid route for characterizing symbolic capital on PC1 and PC2 across century slices.; If provenance remains unavailable, specify and later report limitations distinguishing pooled loadings from time-slice scores.

**Agent reply (plan)**

I checked all staged files and the task metadata for provenance. There are no analysis scripts, notebooks, or additional study-method notes. `capital.csv` contains 39 century rows and 12 numeric-in-concept capital indicators (stored with comma decimal separators), plus period labels `group` and display colors. The separate annual time-series table contains interpolated versions of these same indicators, but not underlying observations or PCA outputs. Nothing in the staged materials identifies an original PCA implementation, unit of analysis, or scaling convention.

A defensible reproducible analysis using what is staged is a single pooled PCA with the 39 centuries as observations and the 12 capital indicators as variables—not 39 separate PCAs. Parse decimal commas; exclude BCE and categorical/display metadata from the PCA; z-standardize each indicator across the 39 centuries (correlation PCA, a clear choice even though values are already called Z values); fit PCA and inspect both variable loadings and century scores. For house size (`Zhausgr`), daggers (`ZDolch`), and hatchets/swords (`ZAxtSchwert`), report their PC1 loadings as the variable associations with PC1. For “distribution across PC2,” distinguish the variables’ PC2 loadings from centuries’ PC2 scores: show the three PC2 loadings and characterize the spread/range of century scores along PC2, optionally highlighting which periods project toward either end. This does not produce century-specific loadings: a pooled PCA has one set of loadings shared across all slices. If the intended question instead means loadings that vary by century, the staged data do not support that claim; the underlying within-century observations or original PCA outputs would be required.

Important limits: this pooled approach is an explicit fallback, not verified provenance for the study's PCA. It is a data-grounded answer to co-variation across the time series, but temporal autocorrelation and the small sample (39 centuries, 12 variables) limit inferential claims. Values appear precomputed as Z scores, but their reference population is undocumented, so per-variable re-standardization can change results; I propose correlation PCA for comparability and would state it. PCA component signs are arbitrary; orient axes consistently for display (for example, make the sum of the three target variables' PC1 loadings positive if nonzero) and interpret signs relationally, not intrinsically.

**Shared evidence:**

task.json provides descriptions and file paths only, with no PCA code/method details. `capital.csv` printed rows run from BCE 4300 through 500 at 100-year intervals, with fields `ZAxtSchwert`, `ZDolch`, `Zhausgr` etc.; 39 observations, 12 candidate quantitative variables. Annual time_series_data.csv has interpolated feature series but no replicate archaeological observations. Full repository inventory had only task.json and three CSV files.

**Assumptions:** One pooled PCA across century rows is a reasonable fallback for examining indicator covariance over time; Re-standardizing the supplied Z-valued indicators across the 39-slice sample (correlation PCA) is transparent and defensible

**Uncertainties:** Cannot verify whether the question refers to an unpublished/original PCA on underlying observations unavailable here; The intended meaning of distribution across PC2 could refer to variable loadings or time-slice scores; report both distinctly; Original Z-score population/scaling and PCA sign convention are undocumented

### 3. Researcher — next_task

Proceed with the proposed fallback analysis: parse comma decimals, perform one pooled PCA with the 39 century rows as observations and 12 indicators as variables, using transparent across-century z-standardization. Report the PC1 loadings for house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords, and distinguish their PC2 loadings from the century scores' distribution on PC2. Include explained variance and relevant diagnostics, state the analyst-selected scaling and sign convention, and clearly qualify that pooled loadings are shared across centuries rather than century-specific. Do not infer an original PCA method that the staged materials do not document.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** approach_review; **approach approved:** True

**Basis:** The inspected materials contain 39 century observations and 12 candidate indicators but no replicate within-century observations, PCA outputs, scripts, or method provenance. The pooled PCA is the explicit fallback proposed to resolve the per-slice identification issue; no empirical findings have yet been reported.

**Open objectives:** Estimate and report target symbolic-capital PC1 and PC2 loadings from the proposed pooled PCA; Characterize the 39 century scores' distribution across PC2 without conflating scores and loadings; Report variance diagnostics, scaling/sign conventions, and limits of the fallback analysis

**Agent reply (result)**

Using the requested fallback, I performed one pooled PCA with the 39 hundred-year slices as rows and the 12 capital indicators as features. Decimal commas were converted to decimal points, and each feature was re-standardized across the 39 slices (correlation PCA; analyst-selected because the staged files do not document the original scaling). The 39×12 matrix is complete and BCE steps are uniformly 100 years.

With PC1 oriented so the sum of the three target loadings is positive, house size (`Zhausgr`) has a positive but smaller PC1 loading (+0.225), while daggers (`ZDolch`) and hatchets/swords (`ZAxtSchwert`) have positive loadings of +0.266 and +0.299, respectively. Thus in this pooled analysis all three are positively associated with PC1, with hatchets/swords strongest of these three. Their PC2 loadings are distinct: house size +0.327, daggers +0.074, and hatchets/swords +0.054. PC2 is oriented so the house-size loading is positive. PC1 explains 30.33% of variance and PC2 19.47% (49.79% cumulative).

These loadings are variable-level associations shared by the entire pooled PCA; they do not change century by century. The century-level PC2 scores are a different quantity: across 39 slices they have mean approximately 0, SD 1.548, quartiles −0.937 and +1.046, and range −3.696 to +2.536. The most negative scores are at BCE 4300–4100 (all −3.696; EN group), while the maximum is BCE 3200 (+2.536; MN). Group mean scores range from −1.139 (YBA) to +1.826 (MN), though group variation is substantial in EN and OBA. Accordingly, the three features’ loadings characterize how they contribute to the pooled PC2 axis; the score distribution characterizes how centuries project onto that axis.

Diagnostics: no missing/nonfinite indicator entries after parsing; all standardized features have population SD 1. PC1 and PC2 loading vectors have unit norm and are orthogonal (cross-product about −1.5e−17). Component signs are arbitrary: the stated positive/negative values rely on the explicit orientation conventions above and should be read relationally. This is an analyst-run pooled PCA on staged Z-valued indicators, not a claim about the study’s undocumented original PCA. The data provide no basis for century-specific loadings or a separate conventional PCA within each century.

**Shared evidence:**

Executed analysis/pooled_capital_pca.py. Output confirms a complete 39×12 matrix; BCE 500–4300 with 100-year steps; standardized feature means max absolute 5.69e-17 and SDs 1. Explained variance PC1 0.3033 and PC2 0.1947. Target loadings: hatchets/swords (+0.298602,+0.053953), daggers (+0.265566,+0.074203), house size (+0.225080,+0.327440), with tuples PC1/PC2. PC2 century scores n=39, SD=1.5483, min −3.6956 at BCE 4300/4200/4100 and max +2.5357 at BCE 3200; loading vectors have unit norms and orthogonality cross-product ~−1.54e−17.

**Assumptions:** Across-century z-standardization is the transparent analyst-selected scaling, not known to match source study choices; A pooled PCA is the requested defensible fallback given one row per century

**Uncertainties:** The original PCA method and underlying observations, if any, are not staged; results do not establish what an original study PCA found; Scores depend on the chosen PCA scaling and sign orientation; PC2 group summaries are descriptive, not inferential, and time slices may be autocorrelated

### 4. Researcher — conclude

The pooled analysis answers the question within the agreed fallback design. In the 39-slice correlation PCA, all three symbolic-capital indicators have positive PC1 loadings: hatchets/swords +0.299, daggers +0.266, and house size +0.225. On PC2, house size is most prominent (+0.327), whereas daggers (+0.074) and hatchets/swords (+0.054) are near zero by comparison. Century scores are distributed across PC2 from −3.696 to +2.536, with the most negative scores at BCE 4300–4100 and the maximum at BCE 3200. These are pooled variable loadings and century scores, not century-specific loadings; the PCA scaling and sign orientation are explicit analyst choices, and the original study method is undocumented.

**Decision:** stop

**Review phase:** result_review; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The reported workflow uses all 39 complete century rows and 12 indicators, with decimal parsing and across-century standardization. Diagnostics report PC1/PC2 variance explained of 30.33%/19.47%, valid orthogonal loading vectors, and the requested loadings and PC2 score range. The response explicitly qualifies the fallback design and sign arbitrariness.

**Open objectives:** None

BCE runs from 4300 to 500 in 100-year increments (no duplicated BCE values). Every cell is populated, but the indicator columns are currently parsed as strings because decimals use commas. The two metadata columns group/color are described as dropped after loading and are not quantitative PCA inputs.\\n\\n`time_series_data.csv` (3,601 \\u00d7 37) has annual CE/calBP records with raw and interpolated versions of the capital indicators, among other series. Raw capital columns have 3,564 missing values apiece, while their `_inter` counterparts are complete\\u2014consistent with sparse source measurements plus interpolation. `pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv` has 4,084 \\u00d7 13 rows and substantially sparse raw pollen PC1 columns; interpolated/smoothed series are complete. No scripts or notebooks are present in the inspected repository. Thus `capital.csv` appears to be the direct time-slice table relevant to the question, while the other files can provide cross-check/context rather than substitute silently for it.\\n\\nProposed concise workflow after approval: parse decimal commas in the capital indicators as numeric; exclude BCE/group/color; retain the 12 indicators and process each row/time slice independently. For each 100-year row, run PCA across the indicators (the relevant interpretation is variable loadings, not observation scores), after choosing a transparent scaling method. Record the PC1 loading for house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords and the PC2 loadings for those variables, then summarize signs, relative magnitudes/rank, and whether PC2 loadings are positive/negative or near zero across slices. Verify numeric conversion, per-slice sample/feature structure, component variance and loading normalization; compare standardized (correlation PCA) with unstandardized PCA if preprocessing is undocumented.\\n\\nAssumptions/uncertainties: the dataset indicates all values are Z values, but it does not specify whether the original PCA used these directly, standardized each slice again, or used interpolated annual series. Since capital.csv is complete and indexed at 100-year intervals, it is the likely intended input, but the 12 indicators may be pre-standardized across the full chronology rather than per slice. With just one row per slice, a conventional PCA treating rows as observations within each slice is impossible; the plausible alternative is PCA on the 12 capital-form values within a slice, which yields loadings for the variables but has only one observation and is not statistically identified as conventional sample PCA. The question wording and data layout therefore leave the PCA unit/organization materially uncertain; analysis should check for relevant code or context if available. Component signs are mathematically arbitrary and may flip independently across slices, so report relative sign patterns only after a consistent sign convention (e.g. orient PC1 to positive sum of loadings) and note this convention. {
  "id": "call_6f0ZmDm0ebT883AXjIlhUhlC",

  "arguments": "{\"stage\": \"plan\", \"summary\": \"Clarify feasible pooled PCA design and provenance limits\", \"answer\": \"I checked all staged files and the task metadata for provenance. There are no analysis scripts, notebooks, or additional study-method notes. `capital.csv` contains 39 century rows and 12 numeric-in-concept capital indicators (stored with comma decimal separators), plus period labels `group` and display colors. The separate annual time-series table contains interpolated versions of these same indicators, but not underlying observations or PCA outputs. Nothing in the staged materials identifies an original PCA implementation, unit of analysis, or scaling convention.\\n\\nA defensible reproducible analysis using what is staged is a single pooled PCA with the 39 centuries as observations and the 12 capital indicators as variables\\u2014not 39 separate PCAs. Parse decimal commas; exclude BCE and categorical/display metadata from the PCA; z-standardize each indicator across the 39 centuries (correlation PCA, a clear choice even though values are already called Z values); fit PCA and inspect both variable loadings and century scores. For house size (`Zhausgr`), daggers (`ZDolch`), and hatchets/swords (`ZAxtSchwert`), report their PC1 loadings as the variable associations with PC1. For \\u201cdistribution across PC2,\\u201d distinguish the variables\\u2019 PC2 loadings from centuries\\u2019 PC2 scores: show the three PC2 loadings and characterize the spread/range of century scores along PC2, optionally highlighting which periods project toward either end. This does not produce century-specific loadings: a pooled PCA has one set of loadings shared across all slices. If the intended question instead means loadings that vary by century, the staged data do not support that claim; the underlying within-century observations or original PCA outputs would be required.\\n\\nImportant limits: this pooled approach is an explicit fallback, not verified provenance for the study's PCA. It is a data-grounded answer to co-variation across the time series, but temporal autocorrelation and the small sample (39 centuries, 12 variables) limit inferential claims. Values appear precomputed as Z scores, but their reference population is undocumented, so per-variable re-standardization can change results; I propose correlation PCA for comparability and would state it. PCA signs are otherwise arbitrary.\\ntarget_indices = [FEATURES.index(v) for v in TARGETS.values()]\\nif loadings[target_indices, 0].sum() < 0:\\n    loadings[:, 0] *= -1\\nif loadings[FEATURES.index('Zhausgr'), 1] < 0:\\n    loadings[:, 1] *= -1\\nscores = Z @ loadings\\nratio = eigvals / eigvals.sum()\\n\\nprint(f'Rows x features: {X.shape}; missing/nonfinite: {np.size(X)-np.isfinite(X).sum()}')\\nprint(f'BCE range: {df.BCE.astype(int).min()}\\u2013{df.BCE.astype(int).max()}; steps: {sorted(df.BCE.astype(int).diff().dropna().abs().unique().tolist())}')\\nprint(f'Z-scale check: largest abs feature mean={np.abs(Z.mean(0)).max():.3g}; feature population SD range={Z.std(0).min():.3f}\\u2013{Z.std(0).max():.3f}')\\nprint('Explained variance ratios PC1-PC5:', ', '.join(f'{x:.4f}' for x in ratio[:5]))\\nprint(f'Cumulative PC1-PC2: {ratio[:2].sum():.4%}')\\nprint('Target variable loadings (pooled, unit eigenvectors):')\\nfor name, var in TARGETS.items():\\n    i = FEATURES.index(var)\\n    print(f'  {name:17s} PC1={loadings[i,0]:+.6f} PC2={loadings[i,1]:+.6f}')\\n\\ns = pd.Series(scores[:, 1], name='PC2 score')\\nprint('Century-level PC2 score distribution:')\\nprint(s.describe().to_string())\\nscore_df = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': df.BCE.astype(int), 'group': df.group, 'PC2': scores[:, 1]})\\nlo, hi = score_df.loc[score_df.PC2.idxmin()], score_df.loc[score_df.PC2.idxmax()]\\nprint(f\\\"PC2 score minimum: BCE {lo.BCE}, group {lo.group}, score {lo.PC2:+.6f}\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"PC2 score maximum: BCE {hi.BCE}, group {hi.group}, score {hi.PC2:+.6f}\\\")\\nprint('PC2 by group:')\\nprint(score_df.groupby('group').agg(n=('BCE','size'), mean=('PC2','mean'), min=('PC2','min'), max=('PC2','max')).to_string(float_format=lambda x: f'{x:+.4f}'))\\nprint(f'Loading diagnostics: norms PC1/PC2={np.linalg.norm(loadings[:,:2],axis=0)}; cross-product={loadings[:,:2].T @ loadings[:,:2]}')\\nPY\\npython analysis/pooled_capital_pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}",

The pooled analysis answers the question within the agreed fallback design. In the 39-slice correlation PCA, all three symbolic-capital indicators have positive PC1 loadings: hatchets/swords +0.299, daggers +0.266, and house size +0.225. {
  "command": "python - <<'PY'\nimport pandas as pd,numpy as np\nD=pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv',dtype=str)\ncols=['ZAxtSchwert','ZDolch','Zhausgr','ZCU_AU','Zamber','ZMonument','ZHort','ZSichel','ZBeil','ZMW','ZKeform','Zkeverz']\nfor c in cols:D[c]=pd.to_numeric(D[c].str.replace(',','.',regex=False))\nX=D[cols].to_numpy(float); means=X.mean(0); sd=X.std(0,ddof=0); Z=(X-means)/sd\nC=np.cov(Z,rowvar=False,ddof=1); vals,V=np.linalg.eigh(C); order=vals.argsort()[::-1]; vals=vals[order]; V=V[:,order]\n# eigvecs are variable-PC loadings; signs: PC1 target sum positive; PC2 house positive\nidx={k:cols.index(v) for k,v in {'Hatchets/swords':'ZAxtSchwert','Daggers':'ZDolch','House size':'Zhausgr'}.items()}\nif V[[idx['Hatchets/swords'],idx['Daggers'],idx['House size']],0].sum()<0:V[:,0]*=-1\nif V[idx['House size'],1]<0:V[:,1]*=-1\nscores=Z@V\nprint('shape/miss',X.shape,np.isnan(X).sum())\nprint('variable mean std min/max',means.min(),means.max(),sd.min(),sd.max())\nprint('EVR',vals[:6]/vals.sum(),'cum2',sum(vals[:2])/sum(vals))\nprint('targets')\nfor k,i in idx.items():print(k, 'PC1',V[i,0], 'PC2',V[i,1])\nprint('all loadings')\nfor i in range(12):print(cols[i],round(V[i,0],6),round(V[i,1],6))\nprint('score PC2 describe',pd.Series(scores[:,1]).describe().to_string())\nsc=pd.DataFrame({'BCE':D.BCE.astype(int),'group':D.group,'PC1':scores[:,0],'PC2':scores[:,1]})\nprint('PC2 endpoints',sc.loc[sc.PC2.idxmin()].to_dict(),sc.loc[sc.PC2.idxmax()].to_dict())\nprint('PC2 group')\nprint(sc.groupby('group').agg(n=('BCE','size'),bce_lo=('BCE','min'),bce_hi=('BCE','max'),mean_PC2=('PC2','mean'),min_PC2=('PC2','min'),max_PC2=('PC2','max')).to_string())\nprint('PC2 scores sorted')\nprint(sc[['BCE','group','PC2']].sort_values('PC2').to_string(index=False))\nprint('orthog',V[:,:2].T@V[:,:2], 'norms',np.linalg.norm(V[:,:2],axis=0))\nPY",

{
  "command": "mkdir -p analysis; cat > analysis/pooled_capital_pca.py <<'PY'\n\"\"\"Pooled PCA of century-level capital indicators from staged capital.csv.\n\nThe PCA is across 39 centuries (observations) x 12 capital indicators (features).\nValues are decimal-comma parsed and re-standardized across centuries (correlation PCA).\n\"\"\"\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\n\nDATA = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / 'data' / 'capital.csv'\nFEATURES = ['ZAxtSchwert', 'ZDolch', 'Zhausgr', 'ZCU_AU', 'Zamber',\n            'ZMonument', 'ZHort', 'ZSichel', 'ZBeil', 'ZMW', 'ZKeform', 'Zkeverz']\nTARGETS = {'Hatchets/swords': 'ZAxtSchwert', 'Daggers': 'ZDolch', 'House size': 'Zhausgr'}\n\ndf = pd.read_csv(DATA, dtype=str)\nfor col in FEATURES:\n    df[col] = pd.to_numeric(df[col].str.replace(',', '.', regex=False), errors='coerce')\nX = df[FEATURES].to_numpy(dtype=float)\nif X.shape != (39, 12) or not np.isfinite(X).all():\n    raise ValueError(f'Expected complete 39x12 matrix; got {X.shape}')\n\n# Across-century z standardization, ddof=0; eigendecompose sample correlation matrix.\nmu = X.mean(axis=0)\nsigma = X.std(axis=0, ddof=0)\nif (sigma == 0).any():\n    raise ValueError('Constant feature')\nZ = (X - mu) / sigma\ncov = np.cov(Z, rowvar=False, ddof=1)\neigvals, eigvecs = np.linalg.eigh(cov)\norder = np.argsort(eigvals)[::-1]\neigvals, loadings = eigvals[order], eigvecs[:, order]\n\n# Deterministic, explicit orientation: target PC1-loading sum positive;\n# orient PC2 so HouseSize loading is positive. PCA signs are otherwise arbitrary.\ntarget_indices = [FEATURES.index(v) for v in TARGETS.values()]\nif loadings[target_indices, 0].sum() < 0:\n    loadings[:, 0] *= -1\nif loadings[FEATURES.index('Zhausgr'), 1] < 0:\n    loadings[:, 1] *= -1\nscores = Z @ loadings\nratio = eigvals / eigvals.sum()\n\nprint(f'Rows x features: {X.shape}; missing/nonfinite: {np.size(X)-np.isfinite(X).sum()}')\nprint(f'BCE range: {df.BCE.astype(int).min()}–{df.BCE.astype(int).max()}; steps: {sorted(df.BCE.astype(int).diff().dropna().abs().unique().tolist())}')\nprint(f'Z-scale check: largest abs feature mean={np.abs(Z.mean(0)).max():.3g}; feature population SD range={Z.std(0).min():.3f}–{Z.std(0).max():.3f}')\nprint('Explained variance ratios PC1-PC5:', ', '.join(f'{x:.4f}' for x in ratio[:5]))\nprint(f'Cumulative PC1-PC2: {ratio[:2].sum():.4%}')\nprint('Target variable loadings (pooled, unit eigenvectors):')\nfor name, var in TARGETS.items():\n    i = FEATURES.index(var)\n    print(f'  {name:17s} PC1={loadings[i,0]:+.6f} PC2={loadings[i,1]:+.6f}')\n\ns = pd.Series(scores[:, 1], name='PC2 score')\nprint('Century-level PC2 score distribution:')\nprint(s.describe().to_string())\nscore_df = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': df.BCE.astype(int), 'group': df.group, 'PC2': scores[:, 1]})\nlo, hi = score_df.loc[score_df.PC2.idxmin()], score_df.loc[score_df.PC2.idxmax()]\nprint(f\"PC2 score minimum: BCE {lo.BCE}, group {lo.group}, score {lo.PC2:+.6f}\")\nprint(f\"PC2 score maximum: BCE {hi.BCE}, group {hi.group}, score {hi.PC2:+.6f}\")\nprint('PC2 by group:')\nprint(score_df.groupby('group').agg(n=('BCE','size'), mean=('PC2','mean'), min=('PC2','min'), max=('PC2','max')).to_string(float_format=lambda x: f'{x:+.4f}'))\nprint(f'Loading diagnostics: norms PC1/PC2={np.linalg.norm(loadings[:,:2],axis=0)}; cross-product={loadings[:,:2].T @ loadings[:,:2]}')\nPY\npython analysis/pooled_capital_pca.py",
  "timeout_seconds": 120
}

Human impact or landscape openness, respectively, as reflected in the pollen data, can be used as a demographic indicator based on the assumption that an increasing population density leads to increasing woodland clearance due to an increasing demand for resources including wood, agricultural land and settlement areas. Each sample from the pollen record used in the principal component analysis is absolutely dated and therefore the openness score (PC 1.) can be plotted as a time series, expressing human induced land clearance. The 'Belau_PC1' of pollen data (pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv) has been assumed to reflect openness. Original openness score through 'Belau_PC1', 100-year smoothed openness score, and linear interpolation of openness score have been used to signify growth. The original openness score (Belau_PC1), the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_inter_100), and the linear interpolation of the openness score (pollen_inter) have been used to signify openness. 6. Demographic growth manifests itself in growth set bringing with it a further opening of the landscape. Opening of landscape corresponds to higher growth rates. During the Early Neolithic, we are dealing with a population growth that goes hand in hand with the opening up of vegetation and the cultivation of the landscape. The growth rate is defined as the percentage change of the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_grate_100). 7. ````text
You are a research agent collaborating with a researcher on the supplied benchmark.
Keep its original question, fixed methods, constraints and required outputs unchanged.
First inspect inputs/repository and propose an approach. Before the main analysis or
implementation, yield a stage=plan handoff: describe observations, proposed choices,
assumptions and important uncertainties. Wait for explicit researcher approval.
Then perform the main work and yield a stage=result handoff with the result or design,
concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.

nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

During the Early Neolithic, we are dealing with a population growth that goes hand in hand with the opening up of vegetation and the cultivation of the landscape. sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\np='data/capital.csv'\\ndf=pd.read_csv(p,dtype=str)\\ncols=['ZAxtSchwert','ZDolch','Zhausgr','ZCU_AU','Zamber','ZMonument','ZHort','ZSichel','ZBeil','ZMW','ZKeform','Zkeverz']\\nfor c in cols: df[c]=pd.to_numeric(df[c].str.replace(',','.',regex=False),errors='coerce')\\nX=df[cols]\\nprint('shape',X.shape,'missing',int(X.isna().sum().sum()),'unique counts',X.nunique().to_dict())\\nprint('means max abs',X.mean().abs().max(),'std range',X.std(ddof=0).min(),X.std(ddof=0).max())\\nZ=StandardScaler().fit_transform(X)\\npca=PCA().fit(Z)\\nL=pca.components_.T\\n# choose sign orientation based on sum loadings of targets > 0 for PC1, then set PC2 according to house size positive\\nidx={'Hatchets/swords':cols.index('ZAxtSchwert'),'Daggers':cols.index('ZDolch'),'House size':cols.index('Zhausgr')}\\nif L[[idx['Hatchets/swords'],idx['Daggers'],idx['House size']],0].sum()<0: L[:,0]*=-1\\nif L[idx['House size'],1]<0: L[:,1]*=-1\\nscores=Z@L\\nprint('explained',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:5], 'cum2',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2].sum())\\nprint('loadings')\\nfor name,i in idx.items(): print(name,'PC1',L[i,0],'PC2',L[i,1])\\nprint('all loading top abs PC1')\\nfor i in np.argsort(-np.abs(L[:,0])): print(cols[i],L[i,0],L[i,1])\\nsc=pd.DataFrame({'BCE':df.BCE.astype(int),'PC1':scores[:,0],'PC2':scores[:,1]})\\nprint('PC2 score summary',sc.PC2.describe().to_string())\\nprint('score extrema',sc.loc[sc.PC2.idxmin()].to_dict(),sc.loc[sc.PC2.idxmax()].to_dict())\\nprint('period groups PC2')\\nprint(pd.concat([df[['BCE','group']].reset_index(drop=True),sc[['PC1','PC2']]],axis=1).groupby('group').agg(n=('BCE','count'),BCE_min=('BCE','min'),BCE_max=('BCE','max'),PC2_mean=('PC2','mean'),PC2_min=('PC2','min'),PC2_max=('PC2','max')).to_string())\\nprint('all score sorted PC2')\\nprint(sc.sort_values('PC2').to_string(index=False))\\n# validate orthogonality / norm\\nprint('loading norms',np.linalg.norm(L,axis=0)[:2], means=X.mean(0); sd=X.std(0,ddof=0); Z=(X-means)/sd\\nC=np.cov(Z,rowvar=False,ddof=1); vals,V=np.linalg.eigh(C); order=vals.argsort()[::-1]; vals=vals[order]; V=V[:,order]\\n# eigvecs are variable-PC loadings; signs: PC1 target sum positive; PC2 house positive\\nidx={k:cols.index(v) for k,v in {'Hatchets/swords':'ZAxtSchwert','Daggers':'ZDolch','House size':'Zhausgr'}.items()}\\nif V[[idx['Hatchets/swords'],idx['Daggers'],idx['House size']],0].sum()<0:V[:,0]*=-1\\nif V[idx['House size'],1]<0:V[:,1]*=-1\\nscores=Z@V\\nprint('shape/miss',X.shape,np.isnan(X).sum())\\nprint('variable mean std min/max',means.min(),means.max(),sd.min(),sd.max())\\nprint('EVR',vals[:6]/vals.sum(),'cum2',sum(vals[:2])/sum(vals))\\nprint('targets')\\nfor k,i in idx.items():print(k, 'PC1',V[i,0], 'PC2',V[i,1])\\nprint('all loadings')\\nfor i in range(12):print(cols[i],round(V[i,0],6),round(V[i,1],6))\\nprint('score PC2 describe',pd.Series(scores[:,1]).describe().to_string())\\nsc=pd.DataFrame({'BCE':D.BCE.astype(int),'group':D.group,'PC1':scores[:,0],'PC2':scores[:,1]})\\nprint('PC2 endpoints',sc.loc[sc.PC2.idxmin()].to_dict(),sc.loc[sc.PC2.idxmax()].to_dict())\\nprint('PC2 group')\\nprint(sc.groupby('group').agg(n=('BCE','size'),bce_lo=('BCE','min'),bce_hi=('BCE','max'),mean_PC2=('PC2','mean'),min_PC2=('PC2','min'),max_PC2=('PC2','max')).to_string())\\nprint('PC2 scores sorted')\\nprint(sc[['BCE','group','PC2']].sort_values('PC2').to_string(index=False))\\nprint('orthog',V[:,:2].T@V[:,:2], 'norms',np.linalg.norm(V[:,:2],axis=0))\\nPY\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"

        "name": "execute_command",
        "arguments": "{\"summary\":\"Save and execute reproducible pooled PCA analysis script\",\"command\":\"mkdir -p analysis; cat > analysis/pooled_capital_pca.py <<'PY'\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"Pooled PCA of century-level capital indicators from staged capital.csv.\\n\\nThe PCA is across 39 centuries (observations) x 12 capital indicators (features).\\nValues are decimal-comma parsed and re-standardized across centuries (correlation PCA).\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport pandas as pd\\n\\nDATA = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / 'data' / 'capital.csv'\\nFEATURES = ['ZAxtSchwert', 'ZDolch', 'Zhausgr', 'ZCU_AU', 'Zamber',\\n            'ZMonument', 'ZHort', 'ZSichel', 'ZBeil', 'ZMW', 'ZKeform', 'Zkeverz']\\nTARGETS = {'Hatchets/swords': 'ZAxtSchwert', 'Daggers': 'ZDolch', 'House size': 'Zhausgr'}\\n\\ndf = pd.read_csv(DATA, dtype=str)\\nfor col in FEATURES:\\n    df[col] = pd.to_numeric(df[col].str.replace(',', '.', regex=False), errors='coerce')\\nX = df[FEATURES].to_numpy(dtype=float)\\nif X.shape != (39, 12) or not np.isfinite(X).all():\\n    raise ValueError(f'Expected complete 39x12 matrix; got {X.shape}')\\n\\n# Across-century z standardization, ddof=0; eigendecompose sample correlation matrix.\\nmu = X.mean(axis=0)\\nsigma = X.std(axis=0, ddof=0)\\nif (sigma == 0).any():\\n    raise ValueError('Constant feature')\\nZ = (X - mu) / sigma\\ncov = np.cov(Z, rowvar=False, ddof=1)\\neigvals, eigvecs = np.linalg.eigh(cov)\\norder = np.argsort(eigvals)[::-1]\\neigvals, loadings = eigvals[order], eigvecs[:, order]\\n\\n# Deterministic, explicit orientation: target PC1-loading sum positive;\\n# orient PC2 so HouseSize loading is positive. PCA signs are otherwise arbitrary.\\ntarget_indices = [FEATURES.index(v) for v in TARGETS.values()]\\nif loadings[target_indices, 0].sum() < 0:\\n    loadings[:, 0] *= -1\\nif loadings[FEATURES.index('Zhausgr'), 1] < 0:\\n    loadings[:, 1] *= -1\\nscores = Z @ loadings\\nratio = eigvals / eigvals.sum()\\n\\nprint(f'Rows x features: {X.shape}; missing/nonfinite: {np.size(X)-np.isfinite(X).sum()}')\\nprint(f'BCE range: {df.BCE.astype(int).min()}–{df.BCE.astype(int).max()}; steps: {sorted(df.BCE.astype(int).diff().dropna().abs().unique().tolist())}')\\nprint(f'Z-scale check: largest abs feature mean={np.abs(Z.mean(0)).max():.3g}; feature population SD range={Z.std(0).min():.3f}–{Z.std(0).max():.3f}')\\nprint('Explained variance ratios PC1-PC5:', ', '.join(f'{x:.4f}' for x in ratio[:5]))\\nprint(f'Cumulative PC1-PC2: {ratio[:2].sum():.4%}')\\nprint('Target variable loadings (pooled, unit eigenvectors):')\\nfor name, var in TARGETS.items():\\n    i = FEATURES.index(var)\\n    print(f'  {name:17s} PC1={loadings[i,0]:+.6f} PC2={loadings[i,1]:+.6f}')\\n\\ns = pd.Series(scores[:, 1], name='PC2 score')\\nprint('Century-level PC2 score distribution:')\\nprint(s.describe().to_string())\\nscore_df = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': df.BCE.astype(int), 'group': df.group, 'PC2': scores[:, 1]})\\nlo, hi = score_df.loc[score_df.PC2.idxmin()], score_df.loc[score_df.PC2.idxmax()]\\nprint(f\\\"PC2 score minimum: BCE {lo.BCE}, group {lo.group}, score {lo.PC2:+.6f}\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"PC2 score maximum: BCE {hi.BCE}, group {hi.group}, score {hi.PC2:+.6f}\\\")\\nprint('PC2 by group:')\\nprint(score_df.groupby('group').agg(n=('BCE','size'), mean=('PC2','mean'), min=('PC2','min'), max=('PC2','max')).to_string(float_format=lambda x: f'{x:+.4f}'))\\nprint(f'Loading diagnostics: norms PC1/PC2={np.linalg.norm(loadings[:,:2],axis=0)}; cross-product={loadings[:,:2].T @ loadings[:,:2]}')\\nPY\\npython analysis/pooled_capital_pca.py\",\"timeout_seconds\":120}"
      }
    }
  ]
}

concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.

Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.

nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

PCA signs are otherwise arbitrary.\\ntarget_indices = [FEATURES.index(v) for v in TARGETS.values()]\\nif loadings[target_indices, 0].sum() < 0:\\n    loadings[:, 0] *= -1\\nif loadings[FEATURES.index('Zhausgr'), 1] < 0:\\n    loadings[:, 1] *= -1\\nscores = Z @ loadings\\nratio = eigvals / eigvals.sum()\\n\\nprint(f'Rows x features: {X.shape}; missing/nonfinite: {np.size(X)-np.isfinite(X).sum()}')\\nprint(f'BCE range: {df.BCE.astype(int).min()}\\u2013{df.BCE.astype(int).max()}; steps: {sorted(df.BCE.astype(int).diff().dropna().abs().unique().tolist())}')\\nprint(f'Z-scale check: largest abs feature mean={np.abs(Z.mean(0)).max():.3g}; feature population SD range={Z.std(0).min():.3f}\\u2013{Z.std(0).max():.3f}')\\nprint('Explained variance ratios PC1-PC5:', ', '.join(f'{x:.4f}' for x in ratio[:5]))\\nprint(f'Cumulative PC1-PC2: {ratio[:2].sum():.4%}')\\nprint('Target variable loadings (pooled, unit eigenvectors):')\\nfor name, var in TARGETS.items():\\n    i = FEATURES.index(var)\\n    print(f'  {name:17s} PC1={loadings[i,0]:+.6f} PC2={loadings[i,1]:+.6f}')\\n\\ns = pd.Series(scores[:, 1], name='PC2 score')\\nprint('Century-level PC2 score distribution:')\\nprint(s.describe().to_string())\\nscore_df = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': df.BCE.astype(int), 'group': df.group, 'PC2': scores[:, 1]})\\nlo, hi = score_df.loc[score_df.PC2.idxmin()], score_df.loc[score_df.PC2.idxmax()]\\nprint(f\\\"PC2 score minimum: BCE {lo.BCE}, group {lo.group}, score {lo.PC2:+.6f}\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"PC2 score maximum: BCE {hi.BCE}, group {hi.group}, score {hi.PC2:+.6f}\\\")\\nprint('PC2 by group:')\\nprint(score_df.groupby('group').agg(n=('BCE','size'), mean=('PC2','mean'), min=('PC2','min'), max=('PC2','max')).to_string(float_format=lambda x: f'{x:+.4f}'))\\nprint(f'Loading diagnostics: norms PC1/PC2={np.linalg.norm(loadings[:,:2],axis=0)}; cross-product={loadings[:,:2].T @ loadings[:,:2]}')\\nPY\\npython analysis/pooled_capital_pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"
